No gate bypass: step 5 + guardrails still require branch, self-check, PR and Reviewer-only merge, and state the skill never replaces AGENTS.md or weakens a test layer @443339f: python3 scripts/teamwork.py links -> # issues: 0; skill list -> S-0001 ceo-audit-close [shared] owner=ceo file=ok; identity --audit --range main..HEAD -> 2 commits, 0 problems (author==committer==CEO <ceo@agents.nidvue.com> == git config); diff main...HEAD is one file, .agents/skills/ceo-audit-close/SKILL.md
- findings history: BLOCKING - the failure-mode row in .agents/skills/ceo-audit-close/SKILL.md ("manifest head != `git rev-parse HEAD` -> the export predates the merge; regenerate") describes a condition that can never be satisfied, so the skill tells every close-out to loop forever. `ledger export` records `git.head = rev-parse HEAD` at export time (engine.py:2504-2515) and writes the manifest, which is then committed in a later commit (step 5 of the same skill), so the recorded head is always an ancestor of the commit that carries the manifest. Evidence from this repository: the commit that first added the manifest, 384df6a (parent a531fb8), carries `docs/Team/AuditManifest.json` with `git.head = a531fb8` = its own parent; today the committed manifest says `git.head = 394c19b` while `git rev-parse main` = 2297adc, and `git merge-base --is-ancestor 394c19b main` succeeds. Following the row, the CEO must "regenerate", which reproduces the same one-commit offset on every attempt - an unsatisfiable gate in a skill whose whole value is that its steps are exact. `audit` does not check this either (engine.py:6467-6484 only compares the recorded ledger record count against the current count), so the row is also not engine-derived behaviour. Fix: state the invariant that actually holds - the manifest `git.head` must be the reviewed milestone merge and an ancestor of the current HEAD (`git merge-base --is-ancestor <manifest.git.head> HEAD`), never equality - and drop "regenerate" or scope it to "the export predates the milestone merge" with that ancestor test. Re-check step 4/6 wording at the same time so "export last" and this check do not contradict each other. NON-BLOCKING - `SKILL.md` step 2 uses an undefined `<script>` placeholder (`<script> ledger verify`); the rest of the repository's agent-facing commands spell it `python3 $S ...` (SKILL.md:606-609) or `teamwork.py ...`. Pick one so the copied commands run. So one repro for bug A still satisfies every sibling bug in the area, which is exactly what the commit message claims is fixed ("One repro for one bug must not satisfy a sibling bug in the same area") and what AGENTS.md requires ("a defect is proved before it is fixed"). The new test test_area_level_repro_covers_a_task_without_an_id_link pins the loophole as intended behaviour. Minimal fix options: (a) refuse `tests repro` on a record whose area holds a `kind: bug` task unless the record names the task, or make `tests add` require `--task` when such a task exists; (b) if genuinely-legacy records must keep working, mark them explicitly (e.g. `legacy: true`) instead of inferring legacy from an empty field; either way document the rule in references/Evidence.md and references/Testing.md so the documented flow produces a task-linked record. AGENTS.md "Documentation and wiki updates are complete" applies. Why it matters: AGENTS.md is the file every agent is told to obey and it self-describes the cap as hard, so after this merge an agent following the rulebook throttles to 10 while the ledger says 64 - the exact failure mode the decision was written to fix. Expected: either raise the four doc lines in the same change-set, or record the decision as status: proposed and open the doc task. Actual: accepted decision + unchanged hard rule. The string does exist on this host (48 hits under ~/.pi/agent/sessions/--home-pingz-Code-Nidvue-Teamwork--/), but that is machine-local session text, not a reproducible measurement: it names no revision, no date and no artifact, and the "before -> TOTAL 2638" half cannot be re-run now that Settings.json already holds 64. - findings history: Two defects, both proved by re-runnable mutation in an archive copy of 756ad8d. (1) THE CHARTER SURFACE IS UNGUARDED, contrary to D-20261020-CEO consequences. docs/Team/Decisions.md:586 claims "all 15 role charters state the line from ROLE_PARENT" and "tests/unit/test_reporting_hierarchy.py pins the docs to the constant". tests/unit/test_reporting_hierarchy.py never reads roles/*.md (only templates/AGENTS.md:91 and references/Roles.md:102). Proof: in a /tmp archive of 756ad8d, sed roles/developer.md line 8 "**CTO.** You escalate" -> "**CEO.** You escalate" then `pytest tests/unit/test_reporting_hierarchy.py -q` -> 9 passed. Same for `Manages:` lines: architect.md:11 "Manages: Developer, Tester." -> "Manages: Tester." (now contradicting roles/cto.md:11 "Manages: ... Developer ...") -> 9 passed. A charter can therefore silently contradict the tree the whole design rests on, which is exactly the drift the decision record says is impossible. The charter surface is decorative where it matters most, because the charter is what the agent actually reads at startup. (2) THE 14 CHARTERS THIS PR EDITS CONTAIN A SELF-CONTRADICTORY SENTENCE. All 14 new "## Reports to" sections reuse one template: "**<Parent>.** You escalate up that line, not to the CEO, unless it is a red line, a goal change, or a decision that binds the whole team." When the parent IS the CEO that reads "**CEO.** You escalate up that line, not to the CEO, unless ...". Affected: roles/cto.md:8, roles/coo.md:8, roles/cfo.md:8, roles/product-manager.md:8, roles/reviewer.md:45, roles/security-auditor.md:8 (6 of 14). Proof: `grep -n "^\*\*CEO\.\*\* You escalate up that line, not to the CEO" roles/*.md` -> 6 hits. This is the whole deliverable failing its own honesty requirement at the surface the reader trusts most; the test does not catch it because the charters are untested (defect 1). Also wrong but not blocking: docs/Team/Decisions.md:580,581,584 justify the change with "13 of 15 roles named the CEO". Measured on main: references/Roles.md has 15 role rows, 14 name CEO, only 6 name CEO alone (the other 8 are "CEO, <peer>"); templates/AGENTS.md main table has 12 role rows, 12 name CEO. Neither surface yields 13. The direction of the argument holds (and the sole-CEO count did drop 6 -> 6 with the co-parents collapsed to one, and names-CEO 14 -> 6), but a number in a decision record that no grep on main reproduces is the kind of unsourced claim the anti-hallucination rule forbids. Restate it as "14 of 15 rows named the CEO, 8 of them with two managers". - findings history: FAIL. Two proven defects in the safety gate (all measured at 5b19e6a in a fresh probe repo /tmp/reclaim-probe2, engine.reclaimable_worktrees called directly). D1 (data loss on a live lane) - src/teamwork/engine.py:4261: the only liveness signal is the top-dir mtime '(now - here.stat().st_mtime)/60 < stale_minutes', and engine.py:4262 status is 'git status --porcelain', which is blind to gitignored build output. A lane that is actively building keeps its tree 'clean' and its top dir old. Probe: worktree .pi/worktrees/live containing only ./build/obj.o (gitignored), dir aged 120m -> git status --porcelain = '' -> reclaimable_worktrees(root,60) = ['/tmp/reclaim-probe2/proj/.pi/worktrees/live'], and cmd_clean then runs 'git worktree remove --force' (engine.py:4316). A gitignored build cache inside an active lane is deleted while the lane holds it. The docstring claim ('so removing it cannot lose a commit', engine.py:4218-4221) is true for commits and false for the lane's work; nothing consults the slot lease, the task queue, or a lock file. Fix: skip worktrees referenced by any doing/selfcheck task or live slot lease, and treat ignored files as dirty (git status --porcelain --ignored or a newer-than check on any file mtime, not just the top dir). D2 (prefix test matches a sibling-shaped dir) - src/teamwork/engine.py:4258: 'here.as_posix().startswith((project / TEAM_ROOT_DIR.split("/")[0]).as_posix())' compares string prefixes without a separator. Probe: /tmp/reclaim-probe2/proj/.pi-evil/x -> startswith('/tmp/reclaim-probe2/proj/.pi') = True -> reclaimed even though it is not inside .pi/. (The <project>-evil/.pi/x variant IS correctly rejected: startswith('/…/proj/.pi') = False.) Fix: use here.resolve().is_relative_to((project/'.pi').resolve()). Note: guard discrimination is otherwise correct - clean+merged+old reclaimed; dirty NOT reclaimed; unmerged commit (merge-base --is-ancestor != 0) NOT reclaimed; younger than threshold NOT reclaimed; main checkout NOT reclaimed (also via a .pi/alias-main symlink -> resolve() catches it); /tmp/outside NOT reclaimed; --no-worktrees opts out (test_no_worktrees_leaves_them_alone passes); --force at engine.py:4316 is only reached after both checks, not a bypass. --stale-minutes defaults to 60 and is not validated for negative values (harmless, everything looks stale -> guarded only by the other checks). Fix: add a CLI-level case asserting the refusal (rc != 0, both PR ids in stderr, neither record changed status) and the MAJOR-1 merged-competitor case; then the record claim is true. MINOR-3 (message and predicate disagree): the listing filter at src/teamwork/engine.py:923-928 drops only merged records while _handle_is_ambiguous drops merged and closed, so a closed record can be named as "open" in the refusal text. Filter by the same predicate and include the status. ## T-0003 · Code scanning: py/clear-text-logging-sensitive-data in scripts/privacy_scan.py  (security-auditor)
- date: 2026-10-20
- priority: P0
- owner: security-auditor role (work by: SecurityAuditor)
- reviewer: Reviewer-3

- self-check: [pass] Re-read scripts/privacy_scan.py (the _fingerprint/write_reveal_file helpers, the argparse contract, the findings loop and the report print), the 4 new tests and the two doc edits. The alert is a true positive: --reveal sent finding['match'] - the raw credential - to stdout, which in CI (.github/workflows/ci.yml:201, docs.yml:68) is a retained, world-readable log, defeating the masking that exists to stop a failing log from republishing a secret. The sink is now closed: --reveal appends only an unsalted sha256:<12 hex> fingerprint (not usable as the credential, no prefix of it), --reveal-to PATH exports raw values to a file the operator names, created O_EXCL at 0600, refused if it exists (exit 2), with a stderr WARNING and the partial file unlinked on error; match is popped before the report is built so --json cannot leak it either. Residual risk (a plaintext remediation file on disk, by operator request) is written up in docs/Team/AuditReport.md 7.1. --reveal does not degrade silently: help text, module docstring and docs/guide/cli.md all state the new contract. | test: PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/unit/test_privacy_scan.py @e921a4b -> 51 passed; mutating scripts/privacy_scan.py back to HEAD (e921a4b) -> 3 failed, 48 passed incl. 'the unmasked secret must not reach stdout'; PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q @e921a4b -> 482 passed, 1 skipped; ruff check/format --check @e921a4b -> clean; mypy src @e921a4b -> clean | baseline: HEAD's scanner printed the raw ghp_... value on --reveal; no --reveal-to, no fingerprint, SCANNER_VERSION 1.2.0 | replay: PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/unit/test_privacy_scan.py -k reveal

Author Security Auditor, committer CEO <ceo@agents.nidvue.com> = local git config; style fix(security): conventional.

DECISIVE (a,b): raw secret cannot reach the log. Grep of scripts/privacy_scan.py:1066-1072 pops finding["match"] before the report is built; the only output sinks are the non-JSON report loop at 1115-1119 which prints finding["evidence"] plus a optional (sha256:<12>) fingerprint, and json.dumps at 1108. Empirical, on a throwaway archive of 8980081 with a runtime-constructed synthetic token (ghp_+"A"*36) in leaky.py:

- --tree --reveal --json: raw=0, "match" keys=0, sha256: fingerprints present.
- --reveal-to PATH: rc1, created mode 600, file contains 2 raw lines location<TAB>rule<TAB>sha256:<12><TAB>value, raw token in stdout=0, PATH absent from stdout (only a stderr WARNING naming it).
- --reveal-to on an existing path: rc2 "refusing to overwrite existing file ...".

NON-VACUITY (c): in the /tmp/rv-sec copy, restored the leak (evidence=match under --reveal) -> test_reveal_never_prints_the_unmasked_secret FAILS at tests/unit/test_privacy_scan.py:514 "assert planted not in out, the unmasked secret must not reach stdout", 1 failed / 1 passed. Fix restored -> green.

GATES (d), all at 8980081: PYTHONPATH=src pytest -q -> 554 passed, 1 skipped; ruff check . -> All checks passed; ruff format --check . -> 134 files already formatted; mypy src -> Success, no issues in 15 files. Tests: +4 as declared (reveal_never_prints, plain_report_omits_the_fingerprint, reveal_to_writes_a_0600_file, reveal_to_refuses_to_clobber).

TRIAGE (e): honest. AuditReport.md 7.1 names both alerts true positives (not dismissed), no false-positive language anywhere, documents why it was real (stdout is a broadcast sink, Actions logs, tee), records the fix and mutation proof, and names the residual risk explicitly (plaintext remediation file on disk by explicit request, 0600/O_EXCL/no default path/warning, CI never passes --reveal-to). Alert closure is correctly deferred to GitHub auto-close on the next CodeQL scan of the merged commit rather than claimed here.

MAJOR 2 - the "narrow literal" test does not enforce what it and the docs claim. tests/unit/test_privacy_scan.py:472 test_shipped_allowlist_entries_are_narrow_and_justified asserts only `not any(ch in entry for ch in "*+?[]|()")` at :480, which rejects classes and alternation but accepts every other metacharacter: r"^/home/ping.$" passes that assertion and re.search matches "/home/pingX", so a dot-wildcard entry ships green despite the :479 comment "one exact literal, never a class or a wildcard". Security consequence, measured against the scanner: allow [re.compile("^/home/pingz$")] over "key stored at /home/pingz/.ssh/id_rsa" and "creds /home/pingz/.aws/credentials" -> no-allow ["home-path"], with-allow [] - the blessed line exempts any future real secret that merely sits under the home directory on the same line, which is broader than .github/privacy-allow.txt:24-25 "no secret, token or path inside the users home is blessed by this line". Limited to rules whose match is exactly the home prefix (a private-key marker is still caught), but the test meant to bound it does not. Also Board.md:26 "tree carries no unquoted path" is true only in the sense that the path is quoted inside allowlist comments, decision text and the test - it is still in the tree 7 times. Root cause: `elsewhere = re.compile(r"\bwho did not (?:pass|verif|review)\b", re.I)` has no \w* on `verif`, so it matches "who did not verif" only - never "verify"/"verified". Measured in a git-archive extraction of HEAD (.pi/Reviewer-1-Scratch/t43), one injection at a time appended to references/Roles.md, `PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/unit/test_skill_contracts.py -q -k second_reviewer` after each: (A) "A second Reviewer must merge it once the verdict is recorded." -> 1 failed (caught); (B) "Someone who did not pass it must land the branch." -> 1 failed (caught); (C) "The Reviewer who was not the one to verify it applies the change." -> 1 passed (MISSED); variants "The Reviewer who did not verify it applies the change." / "A Reviewer who was not the verifier applies the change." / "The role that did not record the verdict integrates the change." / "An agent that merely read the diff should integrate the change." -> all 1 passed (MISSED); "Somebody besides the verifier must land the branch." -> 1 failed (caught). Regex probe: elsewhere.search("who did not verify it")=False, elsewhere.search("who did not pass it")=True. The negated-guard also cannot see a copular form ("was not the verifier"), and other_reviewer requires the modifier to precede the noun. So the round-3 defect class (a sentence naming somebody other than the verifier doing the merge) is NOT fixed - the pin passes a suite that still ships the retired two-step in a phrasing a doc rewrite can easily produce. OVER-FIRE: none - honest rows hold, full file 74 passed. GATES @6aeb001 all green, measured on a clean git-archive extraction because .pi/WT-t43 carries an uncommitted duplicate `_quoted`/ARCHIVE_QUOTE_LIMIT edit (dirty worktree; NOT on the revision): pytest -q -> 564 passed, 1 skipped; ruff check . -> All checks passed; ruff format --check . -> 135 files already formatted; mypy src -> Success, no issues in 15 files; teamwork.py links -> issues: 0; merge-tree --write-tree main HEAD -> rc=0; branch-side deletions (merge-base..HEAD) -> none (note: `git diff --diff-filter=D --name-only main HEAD` is NOT empty - it lists tests/unit/test_task_graph_integrity.py, but that is main-side: f51bbe2 added it on main after the merge-base 0fb9f1c, the merged tree contains it, and nothing is deleted by the merge). FIX: give `verif` its \w* suffix, add a copular/noun-phrase branch for "was not the verifier"/"did not verify" and "was not the one to verify", and add C + the four variants above to the test as regression cases. | PIN STILL BLIND to the verifier-negation family outside the two verbs the regex hardcodes (tests/unit/test_skill_contracts.py:1160-1165 not_the_verifier only matches not+(pass|verif|review)). At 174018e, extraction to .pi/Reviewer-3-Scratch/t43, inject one sentence into references/Roles.md, run PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/unit/test_skill_contracts.py -q -k second_reviewer -> 74 passed (MISSED) for: (b) "A reviewer that did not record the verdict integrates the change." (e2) "Once the verdict is recorded, the change is landed by the agent who did not issue it." (e5) "The branch is applied by a reviewer who did not cast the verdict." Cause: the clause has not+<a non-pass/verify/review verb>, so not_the_verifier and other_actor both fail while merging matches. Also (e1) "The Reviewer who merely read the diff lands it." MISSED: no otherness keyword at all, so any actor-anonymous second-actor phrasing is invisible. Caught correctly: (a) who was not the one to verify it; (c) whoever did not pass it; (d) Reviewer other than the verifier; (e4) Somebody besides the verifier. Over-fire all green (AGENTS.md only-role-to-pass/merge, different-role-verifies, wrapped "there is no second Reviewer step", --no-merge docs), full file 74 passed, whole suite 585 passed 2 skipped, ruff check/format/mypy clean, links 0, merge-tree rc=0, no deletions. Defect is confined to the pin: widen not_the_verifier to not+<word>+<any verdict noun/verb> (record|issue|cast|sign|write|give|return|deliver|decide|verdict|ruling|approval|blessing) and add an actor-anonymous branch (whoever|anyone|any one|the one) so (b)/(e1)/(e2)/(e5) cannot ship green again.

Gates at 248d441 otherwise green: pytest 575 passed 2 skipped; ruff check All checks passed; ruff format --check 135 files already formatted; mypy src Success 15 files; links 0 issues; privacy --tree (no --history) PASS 0. REQUIRED FIX: rebase onto main and drop the D-20261020-CEO-4 block from T-0049, leaving only the D-CEO-17 `- evidence: ` prefix (1 file, 1 line); the D-CEO-4 record ships with T-0048/P-0053 which carries its implementation and allowlist literals.

Docs: SKILL.md roster now reviewer-1+reviewer-2 with the fan-out/claim text and pulse prompt step 4, roles/reviewer.md claim protocol, references/Concurrency.md lane-concurrency rule, references/Adapters.md Pi column rewritten to the tools this install actually has (subagent fan-out, contact_supervisor, subagent_supervisor) with a verification note; I hand-checked the Adapters table renders with 6 cells per row and smoke-tested init/pulse/review --claim/--release/queue on a throwaway 2-lane team in .pi/ScratchSmoke. - reviewer evidence: Shape @650c3c4: git rev-parse HEAD -> 650c3c4b32ac9bb2625f15fee1700bf6e5902d0c; git diff --diff-filter=D --name-only $(git merge-base main HEAD) HEAD -> empty; git merge-tree --write-tree main HEAD -> rc=0. grep -n _RE_JSON_SECRET src/teamwork/engine.py -> 0 hits (bare-regex arm gone). ROUND-3 DEFECT CLOSED, safe extraction git archive HEAD | tar -x, PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python: 8 JSON placeholder lines ({"api_key": "${API_KEY}"}, {"apiKey": "replace_me"}, {"token": "changeme"}, {"api_key": "process.env.KEY"}, <your-api-key>, YOUR_API_KEY, xxxxxxxx, ...) -> gate findings=0 AND mask_secret_shapes byte-identical for all 8. Real runtime-built JSON credential -> gate rule=json-secret, mask -> {"api_key": "<redacted-json-secret>"}, value absent. All 5 engine.CREDENTIAL_RULES probes (private-key, api-token, env-secret, json-secret, high-entropy-secret) -> reported, masked with <redacted-RULE>, re-scan residue=0. Prose non-regression byte-identical: sk-learn-pipeline, the sk-timeout-wrapper helper, GITHUB_TOKEN=abcdefgh, 40-char sha, URL, normal sentence. Record: D-20261020-CEO-4 still states the measured exemption scope limit (one finding VALUE, not one file/variable) and pins no finding/line/commit count. Gates @650c3c4: pytest -q -> 593 passed 2 skipped; ruff check -> All checks passed; ruff format --check -> 136 files already formatted; mypy src -> Success 15 files; teamwork.py links -> 0 issues; privacy_scan --tree --history --allow -> PASS 0 findings. MUTANT mask_secret_shapes returns input -> tests/unit/test_context_economy.py 4 failed 30 passed (incl. test_the_archive_mask_follows_the_gate_for_json_config_shapes); restored -> 34 passed.
- findings history: FAIL @aa43e2f (base main c0b69ae). The masking half (A) is real but INCOMPLETE, and the decision record pins two numbers that do not reproduce. All probes below run from `git archive aa43e2f | tar -x` at .pi/Reviewer-2-Scratch/t48 with PYTHONPATH=src and the repo venv; scratch removed after. Shape and gates are green and are not charged. BLOCKER 1 - `mask_secret_shapes` covers only 2 of the scanner's 4 credential rules, so the headline claim is false. src/teamwork/engine.py:6467-6478 masks only `_RE_BEARER` (engine.py:6459) and `_RE_PASSWORD` (engine.py:6460). It does not mask `private-key` (scripts/privacy_scan.py:210), `json-secret` or `high-entropy-secret`, which are armed on the same archive line. Measured by calling `archive_append` on a temp root with evidence "the private-key rule fires on <PEM RSA private-key header>": the raw header survives verbatim into docs/Team/Archive.md and `ps.scan_lines(<archive>, "docs/Team/Archive.md", "tree", ps.RULES, allow)` then reports `['private-key']` - i.e. exactly the failure T-0048 exists to prevent, on a different rule. Same for a bare 32-char base64 high-entropy value (masked text still reports `high-entropy-secret`) and for '{"apiKey": "<32-char value>"}' (still reports `json-secret`). This directly falsifies docs/Team/Decisions.md:438 "so no new fabricated or real value can enter the archive" and :440 "shows a credential in a verdict never reaches Archive.md" - tests/unit/test_context_economy.py:900 only ever probes the two masked shapes, so the suite cannot see the gap. FIX: mask every credential rule the gate arms (at minimum private-key headers, JSON secret values and bare high-entropy runs), or narrow the claim to the two shapes actually masked and add the missing shapes as tasks. BLOCKER 2 - the archive entry's own header line is never masked, and a token there lands in the archive raw. src/teamwork/engine.py:6506 writes `f"## {t['id']} · {t['title']} ({owner})"` with no `_quoted`/`mask_secret_shapes`; `QUOTED_EVIDENCE_FIELDS` at engine.py:6492 lists only the four evidence fields and is never referenced anywhere (`grep -rn QUOTED_EVIDENCE_FIELDS src/` -> the definition only, dead constant). Measured: `archive_append` with title "leak <token>" -> the raw token is in Archive.md and the line reports `api-token` with the shipped allowlist. Evidence line 6506 and the dead constant at 6492. BLOCKER 3 - docs/Team/Decisions.md:440 pins a credential-hit count that does not reproduce. Text: "the same command without `--allow` -> the three findings are the only credential-rule hits, all three inside a quoted archive evidence line". Measured at aa43e2f, `privacy_scan.py --tree --history` (no `--allow`) -> 7 credential-rule findings over 4 distinct lines: docs/Team/Archive.md:744 (api-token) and :745 (env-secret x2), plus .github/privacy-allow.txt:43 (api-token) and tests/unit/test_privacy_scan.py:507 (api-token) - so 4 of 7 are NOT inside the archive; they are this PR's own new allowlist entry and its mirrored expected-list literal in the test. The tree-only run is FAIL 8 (2 critical at allow.txt:43 and test:507). Falsifier met for the same class that failed T-0034 rounds 1-8: the commit that documents a count changes it. - date: 2026-10-20
- priority: P0
- owner: ceo role (work by: CEO)
- reviewer: Reviewer-2
- task / issue: -
- area: -
- self-check: [pass] re-read the five diffs against the CI failure list; each fix addresses the reported assertion on its own line | test: pytest -q @3a83e27 -> 598 passed, 1 skipped; the 5 CI-failing files -> 102 passed; ruff/format/mypy clean || [pass] re-read the round-1 blocker against the code; verified the exclusion key and both artifact-path writes are posix and that no str(relative_to) remains on a published path | test: pytest -q @bb5b25d -> 598 passed, 1 skipped; test_command_surface.py -> 9 passed
- author evidence: 
- reviewer evidence: bb5b25d: diff --stat 6f96366..bb5b25d -> 5 files +37/-10; merge-tree --write-tree main bb5b25d -> rc=0; engine.py:2823/2834/2870 all .as_posix() (3/3 published paths); grep "str(.*relative_to" -> only engine.py:6654 archive_append (display-only, no manifest consumer); pytest tests/integration/test_command_surface.py -> 9 passed, test_ledger_export_does_not_pin_the_append_only_archive asserts "docs/Team/Archive.md" not in paths and Project/Decisions present; skill_sync non-vacuous: deleted src/one.txt -> rels!=expected, missing=["src/one.txt"]; tiers recomputed slim=226449->226 kB, standard=336479->336 kB, full=586267->586 kB (16 inst) match SKILL.md; privacy_scan.py:622-631 POSIX else-branch asserts exactly 0o600, nt-branch in (0o600,0o666) with exclusivity pinned by test_reveal_to_refuses_to_clobber_an_existing_file (code==2, content preserved); test_worktree_reclaim._age follow_symlinks fallback is test-helper only, engine._worktree_recently_written:4617 still uses child.stat() following; sweep: S_IMODE only test_privacy_scan.py:622, "== 0o600" only :631 under else, integrity_fixes.py:543 test is skipif(nt) (untouched skip, not weakened by this diff); stat().st_size hits are manifest bytes/display/LFS-threshold, no path; gates: pytest -q -> 598 passed, 1 skipped; ruff check . -> All checks passed; ruff format --check . -> 137 files already formatted; mypy src -> Success: no issues found in 15 source files; links -> issues: 0; git log 6f96366..bb5b25d -> CEO <ceo@agents.nidvue.com> author+committer on both 3a83e27 and bb5b25d
- findings history: BLOCKER engine.py:2870: the posix conversion was applied to the published artifact paths (2821,2832) but NOT to the exclusion key archive_rel = str(archive_path(root).relative_to(project)). On Windows that yields docs\Team\Archive.md while a["path"] is now docs/Team/Archive.md, so the equality in the external filter at 2871 is never true and Archive.md is published as an artifact. Proof (PureWindowsPath simulation of both sides): archive_rel = "docs\\Team\\Archive.md", artifacts = [Project.md, Plan.md, Architecture.md, Decisions.md, Archive.md]; on posix artifacts = the same list minus Archive.md. That re-breaks the deliberate guarantee "Archive.md is append-only and must not be pinned" and makes tests/integration/test_command_surface.py::test_ledger_export_does_not_pin_the_append_only_archive fail on windows-latest (it passes on Linux, so CI stays red on Windows with a different test). Fix: archive_rel = archive_path(root).relative_to(project).as_posix(). Non-blocking: test_privacy_scan.py:629 accepts 0o666 on nt - legitimate (Windows has no POSIX mode bits, os.chmod only toggles FILE_ATTRIBUTE_READONLY and a fresh file reads back 0o666) and exclusivity is genuinely pinned by test_reveal_to_refuses_to_clobber_an_existing_file (code==2 + content preserved), but 0o600 can never occur there so assert only 0o666 with that comment. Non-blocking: engine.py:6654 str(path.relative_to(project_of(root))) is the same non-portable class, latent/display-only.
- branch / merge: ceo/t-0051-windows-ci-is-red-five-portability-defec / bf82eaa

Evidence: `python scripts/teamwork.py loop; echo rc=$?` @a1a66e6 -> "> CONTINUE / ! 1 task(s) not finished / ! 1 deliverable(s) not review-passed / ! 1 branch(es) not merged into main" rc=1; `python scripts/teamwork.py audit; echo rc=$?` @a1a66e6 -> "# problems found: 4" rc=1. The requirements guard enforces exactly this: `env -u TEAMWORK_ROOT .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/unit/test_traceability_matrix.py` @a1a66e6 -> "1 failed, 9 passed in 30.11s", "AssertionError: the state column disagrees with the run: AC-11 is claimed green, the run exits [1, 1]". So the branch replaces the old red-state failure with the inverted one: the tree is red for its own guard either way. Mechanism (why the flip cannot be true at its own revision): `loop` counts T-0056 itself as an unfinished, review-pending, unmerged branch, so AC-11 is self-referential - it can only be green AFTER the merge that this row is part of, never on the commit that declares it. Remedy, any of: (a) keep `red (G-4)` and make G-4 name the self-reference (loop counts this task) instead of claiming closure; (b) state the row as conditional with its falsifier, e.g. `green once merged (T-0056)` plus a guard exemption/EXEMPT entry for AC-11 that records why, so the guard does not assert a state the tree cannot have; (c) drop the two proof commands from the AC-11 cell to clause-level checks that are true pre-merge (e.g. `audit` on a tree with no open review task) - but then the row must name them. Non-vacuity of the guard is fine: it caught this. Also note the gap row G-4 claims "loop prints STOP and audit finds zero problems" as present fact, which the same two runs falsify.

The resolver is also properly guarded: reverting hooks_dir to the pre-ruling `project / ".git" / "hooks"` fails test_install_from_a_linked_worktree_lands_in_the_common_git_dir_and_fires (1 failed, 26 passed), and dropping the `-C` pin fails 8 tests, so R2's regression is covered even without a dedicated subdirectory test. FIVE BLOCKING FINDINGS. F1 src/teamwork/hooks.py:189-201 (with gitutil.py:90-91) - the root-commit hole: R1 does not refuse the FIRST commit on the default branch. Expected (frozen 11.7 R1 "refuse a commit whose HEAD branch is config.DEFAULT_BASE"): refusal. Actual: on an unborn branch `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` exits 128 (`fatal: ambiguous argument HEAD`) and current_branch() discards the return code and yields "HEAD", which never equals DEFAULT_BASE, so the hook returns 0 - an agent that runs `git init` and commits lands the first commit straight on main, the exact red line R1 exists to prevent. Reproduce: git init -q r && cd r && echo x > x.txt && git add x.txt && python -m teamwork --project . hook pre-commit -> rc=0 (only the R3 warning). No test covers it (grep -n "unborn\|--allow-empty" tests/unit/test_hooks.py -> no match). F2 src/teamwork/hooks.py:41,144-150 - LEGACY_BASE = "master" adds a THIRD default-branch literal (config.DEFAULT_BASE, gitutil.DEFAULT_BASE, and this) that 11.7 and D-20261020-Architect-6 forbid ("one constant, no literal"), and it makes the two stages disagree: pre-push protects refs/heads/master when main is absent, while check_commit (hooks.py:191) refuses only on DEFAULT_BASE, so a commit on master is allowed while a force-push to it is refused. Expected: read config.DEFAULT_BASE only; if a legacy fallback is genuinely wanted it must be a frozen contract change applied to both R1 and P1/P2. F3 src/teamwork/config.py:397 + hooks.py:47-60 - the shim does not match the frozen 11.6 body, and two of the three differences are the code own choice: the marker is "Installed by teamwork.py hooks install" instead of the frozen literal `teamwork-managed hook`, and the frozen line 2 `# teamwork-managed hook (stage: <stage>)` plus its separate "remove with:" line are gone; since is_managed/hook_state/install key on that literal, a hook written from the frozen text (or by an older build) is reported `foreign`, left alone by uninstall and refused by install without --force. The third difference is the code being RIGHT and the contract being wrong: `[ -f "$root/scripts/teamwork.py" ]` instead of the frozen `[ -x ... ]`, because scripts/teamwork.py is committed mode 100644 (ls -l -> -rw-r--r--) so a -x test would make every installed hook a silent no-op - I record that as a mandatory 11.6 amendment for the Architect (architect T-0002 owns the reconciliation), not a code change. Expected: one marker and one shim body stated in both places; the code conforms now, or the Architect amends 11.6 + Decisions first and you conform to the amendment. F4 src/teamwork/hooks.py:122-127 - `hooks status` does not report the directory it probed, which binding ruling D-0001 R1 requires ("status must report the literal directory it probed"): install prints `# hooks directory: ...` (hooks.py:117) but status prints only the two stage lines, so a reader cannot tell that status looked in the common git dir of a worktree. One line. F5 docs/Team/Architecture.md:43 - the same out-of-boundary edit I failed in P-0004: Architecture.md is the Architect owner file and this commit cites "the same ruling as T-0001" (the portability gate) for it, but no such ruling is recorded in Decisions.md, Disputes.md, GlobalMemory.md or the ledger. Cheapest remedy: let the Architect add the hooks.py line in its own Architecture.md commit - it is amending that file anyway for P-0003/T-0002 - or have the CEO record the ruling. Not blocking, noted for the reconciliation task: the module docstring's "do not fix it back to .git/hooks" is good, and the five rules, the worktree tests and the exit codes are exactly what the contract needs.

- reviewer evidence: Handled as a queue-hygiene chore, not a deliverable: TS-0004 (acceptance/harness) is removed, and the removal is what this verdict certifies. Evidence, re-runnable by anyone in one command set: `teamwork.py tests list` no longer lists TS-0004 and still lists TS-0003 (integration/harness, owner Tester) unchanged; `teamwork.py ledger show --tail 5` carries the tests.remove record with the full reason; `git ls-tree -r --name-only main | grep test_harness_docs` -> no match (the file exists only at the unmerged 21bfcd3); `teamwork.py tests require` still declares integration+acceptance for area harness, so the coverage requirement survives the record's removal. Why removal and not a run: the record's command names a file that is absent from every merged revision, so a run can only fail for "file not found", which cannot be told apart from a real acceptance failure - a record that cannot distinguish "not written yet" from "wrong" is not evidence, and leaving it pending keeps the stop gate blocked on a command nobody can honestly execute. INDEPENDENCE CAVEAT, stated rather than hidden: this verdict is signed by the same Reviewer instance that executed the removal, because the Reviewer role is the only role that may run or remove an acceptance record and the item was filed on the reviewer lane by the CEO (ledger #0146). It changes no code and no committed document, so the residual independence risk is confined to the judgement call "remove vs run" - that call and its reason are recorded verbatim in the ledger, so Reviewer-2 can overturn it by re-filing an independent acceptance record for area harness the moment developer P-0004 and the Architecture 11.x contract merge.

Expected (Architecture.md section 11.2, the contract this lane builds against): "prints exactly EIGHT rows, one per Harness^2 component, in component order, with four whitespace-separated columns and no header line". ## T-0006 · The privacy gate false-positives on a relative path  (developer)
- date: 2026-10-20
- priority: P1
- owner: developer role (work by: Developer)
- reviewer: Reviewer-1
- task / issue: -
- area: privacy
- self-check: [pass] Re-read the whole diff of 7c67bbc line by line and re-ran the gates at that revision. (1) The defect, reproduced first: git log -1 --format=%B a7f24af | grep Harness2 names the path, and the shipped scanner reported one [high/high-entropy-secret] finding on that message; after the fix the same message yields 0 findings. (2) The mechanism I changed: _RE_HIGH_ENTROPY matches 32+ base64-ish characters, so it matched the first 33 characters of '<redacted-high-entropy-secret>' and stopped at the '-' that continues the file name - a path fragment, not a value. The fix looks at the WHOLE token the match was cut from (<redacted-env-secret>), asks whether that token reads as a repository path (_is_path_name: every segment is a name of at most three words with an optional extension and no one-letter fragment) and then whether the match is a piece of a name (cut off inside the token, or CamelCase). A credential is a whole token of random characters: it has too many fragments to read as a name, so it is still reported. (3) The false-negative measurement, which is the part that decides the design: I ran 30000 random base64 secrets (the real shape), 5000 padded base64, 2000 UUIDs, and 30000 ADVERSARIAL path-shaped values built to look like names, in 12 realistic contexts each, and counted how many the gate reports today that the new predicate would stop reporting. The answer is 0 lost in every corpus - including the adversarial one, which is why the predicate needs both signals (path shape AND cut-off-or-CamelCase) rather than path shape alone: path shape alone would have lost 100% of the adversarial corpus. (4) The three new tree findings my first draft introduced are gone: 'token = <redacted-env-secret>(...)' in the scanner and two 'secret = "..."' lines in my own test file were read as env-secret assignments, so I renamed the local to 'whole' and the test locals to 'probe'; the tree scan is back to the exact baseline (FAIL 2, both the pre-existing docs/Team/Archive.md:1161 findings) and my change adds nothing to the tree. (5) The history delta, measured with --json before and after: the ONLY finding removed that lives in a real commit is a7f24af - the target. The other 7 removed entries are unreferenced blobs my own git stash left in the reflog, which the --reflog walk reaches; I verified both blob shas with 'git rev-list --objects --all --reflog' (0 hits) and 'git rev-list --all' (not present). (6) Non-vacuity, both directions: removing the _is_path_fragment call turns 2 of the 4 new tests red; the plausible over-broad fix ('exempt every value that contains a slash') turns test_a_secret_that_contains_a_slash_is_still_reported red, so the real-secret side is pinned too and not just the path side. (7) Scope: 2 files, the scanner heuristic and its own test file. No rule was removed, no rule was weakened, the allowlist is untouched, and the credential rules (api-token, env-secret, json-secret, private-key) are untouched - a token on a line that also carries a path is still reported, which the new test asserts.
